\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \leq \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

the preceding two bounds taken collectively yield

$$
\left| \mathfrak {s} _ {2} \right| \leq \frac {\delta_ {2 r} (2 + \epsilon)}{2 (1 - \epsilon)} \left(\frac {3}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right).
$$

3. the third term  $\mathfrak{s}_3$  can be similarly bounded as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathfrak {s} _ {3} \right| \leq \delta_ {2 r} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq \delta_ {2 r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq \frac {\delta_ {2 r} (2 + \epsilon)}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq \frac {\delta_ {2 r} (2 + \epsilon)}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(\frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {3}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

4. we are then left with the last term  $\mathfrak{s}_4$ , for which we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sqrt {\mathfrak {s} _ {4}} = \left\| (\mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} - \mathcal {i}) \left(\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}} \\ \leq \left\| (\mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}} \\ + \left\| \left(\mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} - \mathcal {i}\right) \left(l _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {f} \\ + \left\| \left(\mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} - \mathcal {i}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where once again we use the decomposition  $\pmb{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} - \pmb{x}_{\star} = \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}_{\star}^{\top} + \pmb{l}_{\star}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top} + \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top}$ . use (50) to see that

$$
\sqrt {\mathfrak {s} _ {4}} \leq \delta_ {2 r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

repeating the same argument in bounding  $\mathfrak{s}_2$  yields

$$
\sqrt {\mathfrak {s} _ {4}} \leq \frac {\delta_ {2 r} (2 + \epsilon)}{2 (1 - \epsilon)} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right).
$$

we can then take the squares of both sides and use  $(a + b)^2 \leq 2a^2 + 2b^2$  to reach

$$
\mathfrak {s} _ {4} \leq \frac {\delta_ {2 r} ^ {2} (2 + \epsilon) ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right).
$$

taking the bounds for  $\mathfrak{s}_1, \mathfrak{s}_2, \mathfrak{s}_3, \mathfrak{s}_4$  collectively yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \leq \left((1 - \eta) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta)\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \\ + \frac {\delta_ {2 r} (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta) \left(\frac {3}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \frac {\delta_ {2 r} (2 + \epsilon)}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \left(\frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {3}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \frac {\delta_ {2 r} ^ {2} (2 + \epsilon) ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly, we can expand the second square in (52) and obtain a similar bound. combine both to obtain

$$
\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \leq \rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \delta_ {2 r}) \operatorname {d i s t} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {f} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}),
$$

where the contraction rate is given by

$$
\rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \delta_ {2 r}) := (1 - \eta) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \delta_ {2 r} (4 + 2 \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta) + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2} + \delta_ {2 r} (4 + 2 \epsilon) + \delta_ {2 r} ^ {2} (2 + \epsilon) ^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2}.
$$

with  $\epsilon = 0.1$ ,  $\delta_{2r} \leq 0.02$ , and  $0 < \eta \leq 2/3$ , one has  $\rho(\eta; \epsilon, \delta_{2r}) \leq 1 - 0.6\eta$ . thus we conclude that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \sqrt {\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} \\ \leq (1 - 0. 6 \eta) \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

# c.2 proof of lemma 2

with the knowledge of partial frobenius norm  $\| \cdot \|_{\mathsf{f},r}$ , we are ready to establish the claimed result. invoke lemma 11 to relate  $\mathrm{dist}(\pmb {f}_0,\pmb {f}_\star)$  to  $\| l_0r_0^\top -x_\star \| _\mathsf{f}$ , and use that  $l_{0}r_{0}^{\top} - x_{\star}$  has rank at most  $2r$  to obtain

$$
\mathrm {d i s t} (\boldsymbol {f} _ {0}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}) \leq \sqrt {\sqrt {2} + 1} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \leq \sqrt {2 (\sqrt {2} + 1)} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}, r}.
$$

note that  $\pmb{l}_0\pmb{r}_0^\top$  is the best rank-  $r$  approximation of  $\mathcal{a}^*\mathcal{a}(\pmb{x}_{\star})$ , and apply the triangle inequality combined with lemma 16 to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}, r} \leq \left\| \mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) - \boldsymbol {l} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}, r} + \left\| \mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}, r} \\ \leq 2 \left\| \left(\mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} - \mathcal {i}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}, r} \leq 2 \delta_ {2 r} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

here, the last inequality follows from combining lemma 15 and (50) as

$$
\| (\mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} = \max _ {\widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n _ {2} \times r}: \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 1} \left\| (\mathcal {a} ^ {*} \mathcal {a} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \leq \delta_ {2 r} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}.
$$

as a result, one has

$$
\mathrm {d i s t} (\boldsymbol {f} _ {0}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}) \leq 2 \sqrt {2 (\sqrt {2} + 1)} \delta_ {2 r} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \leq 5 \delta_ {2 r} \sqrt {r} \kappa \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

# d proof for robust pca

we first establish a useful property regarding the truncation operator  $\mathcal{t}_{2\alpha}[\cdot ]$

lemma 18. given  $s_{\star}\in \mathcal{s}_{\alpha}$  and  $s = \mathcal{t}_{2\alpha}[x_{\star} + s_{\star} - lr^{\top}]$ , one has

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\infty} \leq 2 \left\| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\infty}. \tag {53}
$$

in addition, for any low-rank matrix  $\pmb{m} = \pmb{l}_m\pmb{r}_m^\top \in \mathbb{r}^{n_1\times n_2}$  with  $\pmb {l}_m\in \mathbb{r}^{n_1\times r},\pmb {r}_m\in \mathbb{r}^{n_2\times r}$ , one has

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \langle s - s _ {\star}, m \rangle \right| \leq \sqrt {3 \alpha} \nu \left(\| (l - l _ {\star}) \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {f}} + \| (r - r _ {\star}) \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {f}}\right) \| m \| _ {\mathrm {f}} \tag {54} \\ + 2 \sqrt {\alpha} \left(\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \wedge \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty}\right) \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathbb {f}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\nu$  obeys

$$
\nu \geq \frac {\sqrt {n _ {1}}}{2} \left(\| l \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} + \| l _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty}\right) \vee \frac {\sqrt {n _ {2}}}{2} \left(\| r \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} + \| r _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty}\right).
$$

proof. denote  $\pmb{\delta}_{l} \coloneqq \pmb{l} - \pmb{l}_{\star}$ ,  $\pmb{\delta}_{r} \coloneqq \pmb{r} - \pmb{r}_{\star}$ , and  $\pmb{\delta}_{x} \coloneqq \pmb{l} \pmb{r}^{\top} - \pmb{x}_{\star}$ . let  $\omega, \omega_{\star}$  be the support of  $\pmb{s}$  and  $\pmb{s}_{\star}$ , respectively. as a result,  $\pmb{s} - \pmb{s}_{\star}$  is supported on  $\omega \cup \omega_{\star}$ .

we start with proving the first claim, i.e. (53). for  $(i,j) \in \omega$ , by the definition of  $\mathcal{t}_{2\alpha}[\cdot]$ , we have  $(s - s_{\star})_{i,j} = (-\delta_x)_{i,j}$ . for  $(i,j) \in \omega_{\star} \setminus \omega$ , one necessarily has  $s_{i,j} = 0$  and therefore  $(s - s_{\star})_{i,j} = (-s_{\star})_{i,j}$ . again by the definition of the operator  $\mathcal{t}_{2\alpha}[\cdot]$ , we know  $|s_{\star} - \delta_x|_{i,j}$  is either smaller than  $|s_{\star} - \delta_x|_{i,(2\alpha n_2)}$  or  $|s_{\star} - \delta_x|_{(2\alpha n_1),j}$ . furthermore, we know that  $s_{\star}$  contains at most  $\alpha$ -fraction nonzero entries per row and column. consequently, one has  $|s_{\star} - \delta_x|_{i,j} \leq |\delta_x|_{i,(\alpha n_2)} \vee |\delta_x|_{(\alpha n_1),j}$ . combining the two cases above, we conclude that

$$
\left| \boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \right| _ {i, j} \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \left| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \right| _ {i, j}, & (i, j) \in \omega \\ \left| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \right| _ {i, j} + \left(\left| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \right| _ {i, (\alpha n _ {2})} \vee \left| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \right| _ {(\alpha n _ {1}), j}\right), & (i, j) \in \omega_ {\star} \backslash \omega . \end{array} \right. \tag {55}
$$

this immediately implies the  $\ell_{\infty}$  norm bound (53).

next, we prove the second claim (54). recall that  $s - s_{\star}$  is supported on  $\omega \cup \omega_{\star}$ . we then have

$$
| \langle s - s _ {\star}, m \rangle | \leq \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega} | s - s _ {\star} | _ {i, j} | m | _ {i, j} + \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega_ {\star} \backslash \omega} | s - s _ {\star} | _ {i, j} | m | _ {i, j}
$$

$$
\leq \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega \cup \omega_ {\star}} | \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} | _ {i, j} | \boldsymbol {m} | _ {i, j} + \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega_ {\star} \setminus \omega} \left(| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} | _ {i, (\alpha n _ {2})} + | \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} | _ {(\alpha n _ {1}), j}\right) | \boldsymbol {m} | _ {i, j},
$$

where the second line follows from (55). let  $\beta > 0$  be some positive number, whose value will be determined later. use  $2ab \leq \beta^{-1}a^2 + \beta b^2$  to further obtain

$$
|\langle \boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol{s}_{\star},\boldsymbol {m}\rangle |\leq \underbrace{\sum_{(i,j)\in\omega\cup\omega_{\star}}|\delta_{x}|_{i,j}|\boldsymbol{m}|_{i,j}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{1}} + \frac{1}{2\beta}\underbrace{\sum_{(i,j)\in\omega_{\star}\setminus\omega}\big(|\delta_{x}|_{i,(\alpha n_{2})}^{2} + |\delta_{x}|_{(\alpha n_{1}),j}^{2}\big)}_{\mathfrak{a}_{2}} + \beta \underbrace{\sum_{(i,j)\in\omega_{\star}\setminus\omega}|\boldsymbol{m}|_{i,j}^{2}}_{\mathfrak{a}_{3}}.
$$

in regard to the three terms  $\mathfrak{a}_1, \mathfrak{a}_2$  and  $\mathfrak{a}_3$ , we have the following claims, whose proofs are deferred to the end.

claim 1. the first term  $\mathfrak{a}_1$  satisfies

$$
\mathfrak {a} _ {1} \leq \sqrt {3 \alpha} \nu \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \| \boldsymbol {m} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}.
$$

claim 2. the second term  $\mathfrak{a}_2$  satisfies

$$
\mathfrak {a} _ {2} \leq 2 \| \delta_ {x} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

claim 3. the third term  $\mathfrak{a}_3$  satisfies

$$
\mathfrak {a} _ {3} \leq \alpha \left(n _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \wedge n _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2}\right).
$$

combine the pieces to reach

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle s - s _ {\star}, m \rangle | \leq \sqrt {3 \alpha} \nu \left(\| \delta_ {l} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} + \| \delta_ {r} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}}\right) \| m \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \\ + \frac {\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}}{\beta} + \beta \alpha \left(n _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \wedge n _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

one can then choose  $\beta$  optimally to yield

$$
\begin{array}{l} | \langle s - s _ {\star}, m \rangle | \leq \sqrt {3 \alpha} \nu \left(\| \delta_ {l} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \delta_ {r} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \| m \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ + 2 \sqrt {\alpha} \left(\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \wedge \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \| _ {\mathbb {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

this finishes the proof.

proof of claim 1. use the decomposition  $\pmb{\delta}_{x} = \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} + \pmb{l}_{\star}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top} = \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}_{\star}^{\top} + \pmb{l}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top}$  to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \right| _ {i, j} \leq \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {i, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} + \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {j, \cdot} \right\| _ {2}, \quad \text {a n d} \\ | \delta_ {x} | _ {i, j} \leq \| (\delta_ {l} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} \| r _ {\star} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} + \| l \sigma_ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| (\delta_ {r} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {j, \cdot} \| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

take the average to yield

$$
| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} | _ {i, j} \leq \frac {\nu}{\sqrt {n _ {2}}} \| (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} + \frac {\nu}{\sqrt {n _ {1}}} \| (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {j, \cdot} \| _ {2},
$$

where we have used the assumption on  $\nu$ . with this upper bound on  $|\delta_x|_{i,j}$  in place, we can further control  $\mathfrak{a}_1$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {a} _ {1} \leq \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega \cup \omega_ {\star}} \frac {\nu}{\sqrt {n _ {2}}} \| (\delta_ {l} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} | m | _ {i, j} + \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega \cup \omega_ {\star}} \frac {\nu}{\sqrt {n _ {1}}} \| (\delta_ {r} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {j, \cdot} \| _ {2} | m | _ {i, j} \\ \leq \left(\sqrt {\sum_ {(i , j) \in \omega \cup \omega_ {\star}} \| (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {i , .} \| _ {2} ^ {2} / n _ {2}} + \sqrt {\sum_ {(i , j) \in \omega \cup \omega_ {\star}} \| (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {j , .} \| _ {2} ^ {2} / n _ {1}}\right) \nu \| m \| _ {\mathsf {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

regarding the first term, one has

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega \cup \omega_ {\star}} \| \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2} = \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \sum_ {j: (i, j) \in \omega \cup \omega_ {\star}} \| \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq 3 \alpha n _ {2} \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {i, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = 3 \alpha n _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second line follows from the fact that  $\omega \cup \omega_{\star}$  contains at most  $3\alpha n_2$  non-zero entries in each row. similarly, we can show that

$$
\sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega \cup \omega_ {\star}} \| \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {j, \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq 3 \alpha n _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

in all, we arrive at

$$
\mathfrak {a} _ {1} \leq \sqrt {3 \alpha} \nu \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \| \boldsymbol {m} \| _ {\mathsf {f}},
$$

which is the desired claim.

proof of claim 2. recall that  $(\delta_x)_{i,(\alpha n_2)}$  denotes the  $(\alpha n_2)$ -th largest entry in the  $i$ -th row of  $\delta_x$ . one necessarily has

$$
\alpha n _ {2} \left| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \right| _ {i, (\alpha n _ {2})} ^ {2} \leq \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x}\right) _ {i, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}.
$$

as a result, we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega_ {\star} \backslash \omega} | \delta_ {x} | _ {i, (\alpha n _ {2})} ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega_ {\star}} | \delta_ {x} | _ {i, (\alpha n _ {2})} ^ {2} \\ \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \sum_ {j: (i, j) \in \omega_ {\star}} \frac {\| (\delta_ {x}) _ {i , \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2}}{\alpha n _ {2}} \\ \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \| (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x}) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2} = \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {x} \| _ {\mathbf {f}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last line follows from the fact that  $\omega_{\star}$  contains at most  $\alpha n_2$  nonzero entries in each row. similarly one can show that

$$
\sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega_ {\star} \backslash \omega} | \delta_ {x} | _ {(\alpha n _ {1}), j} ^ {2} \leq \| \delta_ {x} \| _ {\mathbf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

combining the above two bounds with the definition of  $\mathfrak{a}_2$  completes the proof.

proof of claim 3. by definition,  $\pmb{m} = \pmb{l}_m\pmb{r}_m^\top$ , and hence one has

$$
\mathfrak {a} _ {3} = \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega_ {\star} \backslash \omega} | (\boldsymbol {l} _ {m}) _ {i, \cdot} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {m}) _ {j, \cdot} ^ {\top} | ^ {2} \leq \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega_ {\star}} | (\boldsymbol {l} _ {m}) _ {i, \cdot} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {m}) _ {j, \cdot} ^ {\top} | ^ {2}.
$$

we can further upper bound  $\mathfrak{a}_3$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {a} _ {3} \leq \sum_ {(i, j) \in \omega_ {\star}} \| (\boldsymbol {l} _ {m}) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| (\boldsymbol {r} _ {m}) _ {j, \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \sum_ {j: (i, j) \in \omega_ {\star}} \| (\boldsymbol {l} _ {m}) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \alpha n _ {2} \| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {m}\right) _ {i, \cdot} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2} = \alpha n _ {2} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2},
$$

where the last line follows from the fact that  $\omega_{\star}$  contains at most  $\alpha n_2$  non-zero entries in each row. similarly, one can obtain

$$
\mathfrak {a} _ {3} \leq \alpha n _ {1} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {m} \| _ {2, \infty} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {m} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2},
$$

which completes the proof.

# d.1 proof of lemma 3

we begin with introducing several useful notations and facts. in view of the condition  $\mathrm{dist}(f_t, f_\star) \leq 0.02\sigma_r(x_\star)$  and lemma 9, one knows that  $q_t$ , the optimal alignment matrix between  $f_t$  and  $f_\star$  exists. therefore, for notational convenience, denote  $\pmb{l} \coloneqq \pmb{l}_t\pmb{q}_t$ ,  $\pmb{r} \coloneqq \pmb{r}_t\pmb{q}_t^{-\top}$ ,  $\delta_l \coloneqq \pmb{l} - \pmb{l}_\star$ ,  $\delta_r \coloneqq \pmb{r} - \pmb{r}_\star$ ,  $\pmb{s} \coloneqq \pmb{s}_t = \mathcal{t}_{2\alpha}[\pmb{x}_\star + \pmb{s}_\star - \pmb{l}\pmb{r}^\top]$ , and  $\epsilon \coloneqq 0.02$ . similar to the derivation in (45), we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \vee \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \epsilon . \tag {56}
$$

moreover, the incoherence condition

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \tag {57}
$$

implies

$$
\left. \sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\mu r}, \right. \tag {58}
$$

which combined with the triangle inequality further implies

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| l \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| r \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq 2 \sqrt {\mu r}. \tag {59}
$$

the conclusion  $\| l_t r_t^\top - x_\star \|_{\mathsf{f}} \leq 1.5 \, \mathrm{dist}(f_t, f_\star)$  is a simple consequence of lemma 13; see (48) for a detailed argument. in what follows, we shall prove the distance contraction and the incoherence condition separately.

# d.1.1 distance contraction

by the definition of  $\mathrm{dist}^2 (\pmb {f}_{t + 1},\pmb {f}_\star)$  , one has

$$
\left. \operatorname {d i s t} ^ {2} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}. \right. \tag {60}
$$

from now on, we focus on controlling the first square  $\| (\pmb{l}_{t + 1}\pmb{q}_t - \pmb{l}_\star)\pmb{\sigma}_\star^{1 / 2}\| _f^2$ . in view of the update rule (20), one has

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(l _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - l _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} = \left(\boldsymbol {l} - \eta \left(\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} - \eta (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} - \eta (\boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = (1 - \eta) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta (\boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}. \tag {61} \\ \end{array}
$$

here, we use the notation introduced above and the decomposition  $\pmb{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} - \pmb{x}_{\star} = \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} + \pmb{l}_{\star}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top}$ . take the squared frobenius norm of both sides of (61) to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} = \underbrace {\left\| \left(1 - \eta\right) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} _ {\mathfrak {r} _ {1}} \\ - 2 \eta (1 - \eta) \underbrace {\operatorname {t r} \left(\left(s - s _ {\star}\right) r \left(r ^ {\top} r\right) ^ {- 1} \sigma_ {\star} \delta_ {l} ^ {\top}\right)} _ {\mathfrak {r} _ {2}} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} + 2 \eta^ {2} \underbrace {\operatorname {t r} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} ^ {\top}\right)} _ {\mathfrak {n} _ {3}} \\ + \eta^ {2} \underbrace {\left\| (\boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} _ {\mathfrak {n} _ {4}} \\ \end{array}
$$

in the sequel, we shall bound the four terms separately, of which  $\re_1$  is the main term, and  $\re_2, \re_3$  and  $\re_4$  are perturbation terms.

1. notice that the main term  $\re_1$  has already been controlled in (46) under the condition (56). it obeys

$$
\mathfrak {r} _ {1} \leq \left((1 - \eta) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta)\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

2. for the second term  $\re_2$ , set  $m := \delta_l \sigma_\star (r^\top r)^{-1} r^\top$  with  $l_m := \delta_l \sigma_\star (r^\top r)^{-1} \sigma_\star^{1/2}$ ,  $r_m := r \sigma_\star^{-1/2}$  and then invoke lemma 18 with  $\nu := 3\sqrt{\mu r}/2$  to see

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathfrak {r} _ {2} \right| \leq \frac {3}{2} \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ + 2 \sqrt {\alpha n _ {2}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {f} \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {l r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {f} \\ \leq \frac {3}{2} \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ + 2 \sqrt {\alpha n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {f} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {l r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {f}. \\ \end{array}
$$

take the condition (56) and lemmas 12 and 13 together to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{1 - \epsilon}; \\ \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}; \tag {62} \\ \left\| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \leq \left(1 + \frac {\epsilon}{2}\right) \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

these consequences combined with the condition (59) yield

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \re_ {2} \right| \leq \frac {3 \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r}}{2 (1 - \epsilon)} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ + \frac {4 \sqrt {\alpha \mu r}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} (1 + \frac {\epsilon}{2}) \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \\ \leq \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} \frac {3 \sqrt {3} + \frac {4 (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon}}{2 (1 - \epsilon)} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \\ \leq \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} \frac {3 \sqrt {3} + \frac {4 (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon}}{2 (1 - \epsilon)} \left(\frac {3}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality holds since  $2ab \leq a^2 + b^2$ .

3. the third term  $\re_3$  can be controlled similarly. set  $m := l_{\star} \pmb{\delta}_r^\top \pmb{r} (\pmb{r}^\top \pmb{r})^{-1} \pmb{\sigma}_{\star} (\pmb{r}^\top \pmb{r})^{-1} \pmb{r}^\top$  with  $l_m := l_{\star} \pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{-1/2}$  and  $r_m := r (\pmb{r}^\top \pmb{r})^{-1} \pmb{\sigma}_{\star} (\pmb{r}^\top \pmb{r})^{-1} \pmb{r}^\top \pmb{\delta}_r \pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2}$ , and invoke lemma 18 with  $\nu := 3\sqrt{\mu r}/2$  to arrive at

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \re_ {3} \right| \leq \frac {3}{2} \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ + 2 \sqrt {\alpha n _ {1}} \| l _ {\star} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \left\| r (r ^ {\top} r) ^ {- 1} \sigma_ {\star} (r ^ {\top} r) ^ {- 1} r ^ {\top} \delta_ {r} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {f} \| l r ^ {\top} - x _ {\star} \| _ {f} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \leq \frac {3}{2} \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \\ + 2 \sqrt {\alpha n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {f}} \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

use the consequences (62) again to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \re_ {3} \right| \leq \frac {3 \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r}}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ + \frac {2 \sqrt {\alpha \mu r}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} (1 + \frac {\epsilon}{2}) \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}}\right) \\ \leq \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} \frac {3 \sqrt {3} + 2 (2 + \epsilon)}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ \leq \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} \frac {3 \sqrt {3} + 2 (2 + \epsilon)}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(\frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {3}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

4. for the last term  $\re_4$ , utilize the variational representation of the frobenius norm to see

$$
\sqrt {\mathfrak {r} _ {4}} = \operatorname {t r} \left(\left(\boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} ^ {\top}\right)
$$

for some  $\widetilde{\pmb{l}}\in \mathbb{r}^{n_1\times r}$  obeying  $\| \widetilde{l}\|_{\mathsf{f}} = 1$  . setting  $m\coloneqq \widetilde{l}\sigma_{\star}^{1 / 2}(r^{\top}r)^{-1}r^{\top} = l_{m}r_{m}^{\top}$  with  $l_{m}\coloneqq \widetilde{l}\sigma_{\star}^{1 / 2}(r^{\top}r)^{-1}\sigma_{\star}^{1 / 2}$  and  $r_{m}\coloneqq r\sigma_{\star}^{-1 / 2}$  , we are ready to apply lemma 18 again with  $\nu \coloneqq 3\sqrt{\mu r} /2$  to see

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sqrt {\mathfrak {r} _ {4}} \leq \frac {3}{2} \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ + 2 \sqrt {\alpha n _ {2}} \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {l}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {f} \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {f} \\ \leq \frac {3}{2} \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ + 2 \sqrt {\alpha n _ {2}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

this combined with the consequences (62) and condition (59) yields

$$
\sqrt {\mathfrak {r} _ {4}} \leq \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} \frac {3 \sqrt {3} + \frac {4 (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon}}{2 (1 - \epsilon)} \left(\| \pmb {\delta} _ {l} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \pmb {\delta} _ {r} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right).
$$

take the square, and use the elementary inequality  $(a + b)^2 \leq 2a^2 + 2b^2$  to reach

$$
\mathfrak {r} _ {4} \leq \alpha \mu r \frac {(3 \sqrt {3} + \frac {4 (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon}) ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right).
$$

taking collectively the bounds for  $\re_1, \re_2, \re_3$  and  $\re_4$  yields the control of  $\|(l_{t+1}q_t - l_\star)\sigma_\star^{1/2}\|_{\mathbb{f}}^2$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \leq \left((1 - \eta) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta)\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \\ + \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} \frac {3 \sqrt {3} + \frac {4 (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon}}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta) \left(\frac {3}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} \frac {3 \sqrt {3} + 2 (2 + \epsilon)}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \left(\frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {3}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \alpha \mu r \frac {(3 \sqrt {3} + \frac {4 (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon}) ^ {2}}{2 (1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly, we can obtain the control of  $\| (\pmb{r}_{t + 1}\pmb{q}_t^{-\top} - \pmb{r}_{\star})\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1 / 2}\|_{\mathsf{f}}^2$ . combine them together and identify  $\mathrm{dist}^2 (\pmb {f}_t,\pmb {f}_\star) = \| \pmb {\delta}_l\pmb {\sigma}_\star^{1 / 2}\|_{\mathsf{f}}^2 +\| \pmb {\delta}_r\pmb {\sigma}_\star^{1 / 2}\|_{\mathsf{f}}^2$  to reach

$$
\left\| (\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| (\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \leq \rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \alpha \mu r) \mathrm {d i s t} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {f} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}),
$$

where the contraction rate  $\rho^2 (\eta ;\epsilon ,\alpha \mu r)$  is given by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \alpha \mu r) := (1 - \eta) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} (6 \sqrt {3} + \frac {8 (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon})}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta) \\ + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2} + \sqrt {\alpha \mu r} (6 \sqrt {3} + 4 (2 + \epsilon)) + \alpha \mu r (3 \sqrt {3} + \frac {4 (2 + \epsilon)}{1 - \epsilon}) ^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

with  $\epsilon = 0.02$ ,  $\alpha \mu r \leq 10^{-4}$ , and  $0 < \eta \leq 2/3$ , one has  $\rho(\eta; \epsilon, \alpha \mu r) \leq 1 - 0.6\eta$ . thus we conclude that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \sqrt {\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} \\ \leq (1 - 0. 6 \eta) \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right). \tag {63} \\ \end{array}
$$

# d.1.2 incoherence condition

we start by controlling the term  $\| (l_{t + 1}q_t - l_\star)\sigma_\star^{1 / 2}\|_{2,\infty}$ . we know from (61) that

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} = (1 - \eta) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta (\boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}.
$$

apply the triangle inequality to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq (1 - \eta) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} + \eta \underbrace {\left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2 , \infty}} _ {\mathfrak {t} _ {1}} \\ + \eta \underbrace {\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2 , \infty}} _ {\mathfrak {t} _ {2}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

the first term  $\| \pmb{\delta}_l \pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2} \|_{2,\infty}$  follows from the incoherence condition (57) as

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

in the sequel, we shall bound the terms  $\mathfrak{t}_1$  and  $\mathfrak{t}_2$ .

1. for the term  $\mathfrak{t}_1$ , use the relation  $\| ab \|_{2,\infty} \leq \| a \|_{2,\infty} \| b \|_{\mathrm{op}}$ , and combine the condition (56) with the consequences (62) to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {t} _ {1} \leq \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \frac {\epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}), \\ \end{array}
$$

2. for the term  $\mathfrak{t}_2$ , use the relation  $\|ab\|_{2,\infty} \leq \|a\|_{2,\infty} \|b\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  to obtain

$$
\mathfrak {t} _ {2} \leq \| \boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

we know from lemma 18 that  $\pmb{s} - \pmb{s}_{\star}$  has at most  $3\alpha n_{2}$  non-zero entries in each row, and  $\| \pmb{s} - \pmb{s}_{\star}\|_{\infty} \leq 2\| lr^{\top} - x_{\star}\|_{\infty}$ . upper bound the  $\ell_{2,\infty}$  norm by the  $\ell_{\infty}$  norm as

$$
\| \boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {3 \alpha n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {s} - \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \| _ {\infty} \leq 2 \sqrt {3 \alpha n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {l r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\infty}.
$$

split  $\pmb{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} - \pmb{x}_{\star} = \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} + \pmb{l}_{\star}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top}$ , and take the conditions (57) and (59) to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| l r ^ {\top} - x _ {\star} \right\| _ {\infty} \leq \left\| \delta_ {l} r ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\infty} + \left\| l _ {\star} \delta_ {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\infty} \\ \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} + \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \\ \leq \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) 2 \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {2}}} + \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {2}}} \sigma_ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) \\ = \frac {3 \mu r}{\sqrt {n _ {1} n _ {2}}} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}). \\ \end{array}
$$

this combined with the consequences (62) yields

$$
\mathfrak {t} _ {2} \leq \frac {6 \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r}}{1 - \epsilon} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} (x _ {\star}).
$$

taking collectively the bounds for  $\mathfrak{t}_1, \mathfrak{t}_2$  yields the control

$$
\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left(1 - \eta + \frac {\epsilon + 6 \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r}}{1 - \epsilon} \eta\right) \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}). \tag {64}
$$

the last step is to switch the alignment matrix from  $q_{t}$  to  $q_{t + 1}$ . (63) together with lemma 9 demonstrates the existence of  $q_{t + 1}$ . apply the triangle inequality to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} + \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \left(\boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {q} _ {t}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \\ \leq \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} + \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

we deduct from (64) that

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} + \left\| (\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left(2 - \eta + \frac {\epsilon + 6 \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r}}{1 - \epsilon} \eta\right) \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}}.
$$

regarding the alignment matrix term, invoke lemma 14 to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\| (\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} (\boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} ^ {- \top}) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{1 - \| (\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ \leq \frac {\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}}{1 - \| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}} \\ \leq \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \sigma_ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we deduct from (63) that the distances using either  $q_{t}$  or  $q_{t + 1}$  are bounded by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \epsilon \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}); \\ \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \epsilon \sigma_ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right); \\ \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \epsilon . \\ \end{array}
$$

combine all pieces to reach

$$
\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left(\frac {1 + \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \left(1 - \eta + \frac {\epsilon + 6 \sqrt {3 \alpha \mu r}}{1 - \epsilon} \eta\right) + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon}\right) \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

with  $\epsilon = 0.02$ ,  $\alpha \mu r \leq 10^{-4}$ , and  $0.1 \leq \eta \leq 2/3$ , we get the desired incoherence condition

$$
\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

similarly, we can prove the other part

$$
\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t + 1} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {2}}} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

# d.2 proof of lemma 4

we first record two lemmas from [ypcc16], which are useful for studying the properties of the initialization.

lemma 19 ([ypcc16, section 6.1]). given  $s_{\star} \in s_{\alpha}$ , one has  $\| s_{\star} - \mathcal{t}_{\alpha}[x_{\star} + s_{\star}] \|_{\infty} \leq 2 \| x_{\star} \|_{\infty}$ .

lemma 20 ([ypcc16, lemma 1]). for any matrix  $m \in s_{\alpha}$ , one has  $\| m \|_{\mathrm{op}} \leq \alpha \sqrt{n_1 n_2} \| m \|_{\infty}$ .

with these two lemmas in place, we are ready to establish the claimed result. invoke lemma 11 to obtain

$$
\left. \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {0}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \sqrt {\sqrt {2} + 1} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \leq \sqrt {(\sqrt {2} + 1) 2 r} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \right.
$$

where the last relation uses the fact that  $\pmb{l}_0\pmb{r}_0^\top - \pmb{x}_{\star}$  has rank at most  $2r$ . we can further apply the triangle inequality to see

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {y} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \boldsymbol {y} ] - \boldsymbol {l} _ {0} \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \| \boldsymbol {y} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \boldsymbol {y} ] - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq 2 \left\| \boldsymbol {y} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \boldsymbol {y} ] - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = 2 \left\| \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} ] \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

here the second inequality hinges on the fact that  $\pmb{l}_0\pmb{r}_0^\top$  is the best rank-  $r$  approximation of  $\mathbf{y} - \mathcal{t}_{\alpha}[\mathbf{y}]$ , and the last identity arises from  $\pmb{y} = \pmb{x}_{\star} + \pmb{s}_{\star}$ . follow the same argument as [ypcc16, section 6.1], combining lemmas 19 and 20 to reach

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} ] \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 2 \alpha \sqrt {n _ {1} n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} ] \| _ {\infty} \\ \leq 4 \alpha \sqrt {n _ {1} n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\infty} \leq 4 \alpha \mu r \kappa \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last inequality follows from the incoherence assumption

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\infty} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {u} _ {\star} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} _ {\star} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {n _ {1} n _ {2}}} \kappa \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}). \tag {65}
$$

take the above inequalities together to arrive at

$$
\operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {0}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq 8 \sqrt {2 (\sqrt {2} + 1)} \alpha \mu r ^ {3 / 2} \kappa \sigma_ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) \leq 2 0 \alpha \mu r ^ {3 / 2} \kappa \sigma_ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right).
$$

# d.3 proof of lemma 5

in view of the condition  $\mathrm{dist}(f_0, f_\star) \leq 0.02\sigma_r(x_\star)$  and lemma 9, one knows that  $q_0$ , the optimal alignment matrix between  $f_0$  and  $f_\star$  exists. therefore, for notational convenience, denote  $l \coloneqq l_0q_0$ ,  $r \coloneqq r_0q_0^{-\top}$ ,  $\delta_l \coloneqq l - l_\star$ ,  $\delta_r \coloneqq r - r_\star$ , and  $\epsilon \coloneqq 0.02$ . our objective is then translated to demonstrate

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

from now on, we focus on bounding  $\| \pmb{\delta}_l \pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2} \|_{2,\infty}$ . since  $\pmb{u}_0 \pmb{\sigma}_0 \pmb{v}_0^\top$  is the top- $r$  svd of  $\pmb{y} - \mathcal{t}_{\alpha}[\pmb{y}]$ , and recall that  $\pmb{y} = \pmb{x}_{\star} + \pmb{s}_{\star}$ , we have the relation

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \right]\right) \boldsymbol {v} _ {0} = \boldsymbol {u} _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {0},
$$

which further implies the following decomposition of  $\pmb{\delta}_l\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1 / 2}$

claim 4. one has

$$
\pmb {\delta} _ {l} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} = (\pmb {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \pmb {x} _ {\star} + \pmb {s} _ {\star} ]) \pmb {r} (\pmb {r} ^ {\top} \pmb {r}) ^ {- 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \pmb {l} _ {\star} \pmb {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \pmb {r} (\pmb {r} ^ {\top} \pmb {r}) ^ {- 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}.
$$

combining claim 4 with the triangle inequality yields

$$
\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \underbrace {\left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2 , \infty}} _ {\mathfrak {i} _ {1}} + \underbrace {\left\| (\boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} ]) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2 , \infty}} _ {\mathfrak {i} _ {2}}.
$$

in what follows, we shall control  $\mathfrak{i}_1$  and  $\mathfrak{i}_2$  in turn.

1. for the term  $\mathfrak{i}_1$ , use the relation  $\| \boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2,\infty} \leq \| \boldsymbol{a}\|_{2,\infty}\| \boldsymbol{b}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  to obtain

$$
\mathfrak {i} _ {1} \leq \| l _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \delta_ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

the incoherence assumption tells  $\| l_{\star}\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{-1 / 2}\|_{2,\infty} = \| u_{\star}\|_{2,\infty}\leq \sqrt{\mu r / n_1}$ . in addition, the assumption  $\mathrm{dist}(f_0,f_\star)\leq \epsilon \sigma_r(x_\star)$  entails the bound  $\| \delta_r\sigma_\star^{1 / 2}\|_{\mathsf{op}}\leq \epsilon \sigma_r(x_\star)$ . finally, repeating the argument for obtaining (56) yields  $\| \delta_r\sigma_\star^{-1 / 2}\|_{\mathsf{op}}\leq \epsilon$ , which together with lemma 12 reveals

$$
\left. \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{1 - \epsilon}. \right.
$$

in all, we arrive at

$$
\mathfrak {i} _ {1} \leq \frac {\epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} (x _ {\star}).
$$

2. proceeding to the term  $\mathfrak{j}_2$ , use the relations  $\| \boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2,\infty} \leq \| \boldsymbol{a}\|_{1,\infty}\| \boldsymbol{b}\|_{2,\infty}$  and  $\| \boldsymbol{a}\boldsymbol{b}\|_{2,\infty} \leq \| \boldsymbol{a}\|_{2,\infty}\| \boldsymbol{b}\|_{\mathrm{op}}$  to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {i} _ {2} \leq \| \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} ] \| _ {1, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \\ \leq \| s _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} [ x _ {\star} + s _ {\star} ] \| _ {1, \infty} \| r \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\text {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

regarding  $s_{\star} - \mathcal{t}_{\alpha}[x_{\star} + s_{\star}]$ , lemma 19 tells that  $s_{\star} - \mathcal{t}_{\alpha}[x_{\star} + s_{\star}]$  has at most  $2\alpha n_2$  non-zero entries in each row, and  $\| s_{\star} - \mathcal{t}_{\alpha}[x_{\star} + s_{\star}] \|_{\infty} \leq 2 \| x_{\star} \|_{\infty}$ . consequently, we can upper bound the  $\ell_{1,\infty}$  norm by the  $\ell_{\infty}$  norm as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \right] \right\| _ {1, \infty} \leq 2 \alpha n _ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \right] \right\| _ {\infty} \\ \leq 4 \alpha n _ {2} \| x _ {\star} \| _ {\infty} \\ \leq 4 \alpha n _ {2} \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {n _ {1} n _ {2}}} \kappa \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}). \\ \end{array}
$$

here the last inequality follows from the incoherence assumption (65). for the term  $\| r\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{-1/2}\|_{2,\infty}$ , one can apply the triangle inequality to see

$$
\| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {2}}} + \frac {\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2 , \infty}}{\sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star})}.
$$

last but not least, repeat the argument for (62) to obtain

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} = \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}.
$$

taking together the above bounds yields

$$
\mathfrak {i} _ {2} \leq \frac {4 \alpha \mu r \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) + \frac {4 \alpha \mu r \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {\frac {n _ {2}}{n _ {1}}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty}.
$$

combine the bounds on  $\mathfrak{i}_1$  and  $\mathfrak{i}_2$  to reach

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left(\frac {\epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {4 \alpha \mu r \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\right) \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) + \frac {4 \alpha \mu r \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty}.
$$

similarly, we have

$$
\sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \pmb {\delta} _ {r} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left(\frac {\epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {4 \alpha \mu r \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\right) \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {r} (\pmb {x} _ {\star}) + \frac {4 \alpha \mu r \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \pmb {\delta} _ {l} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty}.
$$

taking the maximum and solving for  $\sqrt{n_1}\| \pmb{\delta}_l\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1 / 2}\|_{2,\infty}\vee \sqrt{n_2}\| \pmb{\delta}_l\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1 / 2}\|_{2,\infty}$  yield the relation

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \frac {\epsilon (1 - \epsilon) + 4 \alpha \mu r \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2} - 4 \alpha \mu r \kappa} \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

with  $\epsilon = 0.02$  and  $\alpha \mu r\kappa \leq 0.1$ , we get the desired conclusion

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

proof of claim 4. identify  $\mathbf{u}_0$  (resp.  $\mathbf{v}_0$ ) with  $\mathbf{l}_0 \mathbf{\sigma}_0^{-1/2}$  (resp.  $\mathbf{r}_0 \mathbf{\sigma}_0^{-1/2}$ ) to yield

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \right]\right) \boldsymbol {r} _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {0} ^ {- 1} = \boldsymbol {l} _ {0},
$$

which is equivalent to  $(\pmb{x}_{\star} + \pmb{s}_{\star} - \mathcal{t}_{\alpha}[\pmb{x}_{\star} + \pmb{s}_{\star}])\pmb{r}_0(\pmb{r}_0^\top \pmb{r}_0)^{-1} = \pmb{l}_0$  since  $\pmb{\sigma}_0 = \pmb{r}_0^\top \pmb{r}_0$ . multiply both sides by  $q_0\sigma_\star^{1 / 2}$  to obtain

$$
\left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \right]\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} = \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2},
$$

where we recall that  $\pmb{l} = \pmb{l}_0\pmb{q}_0$  and  $\pmb{r} = \pmb{r}_0\pmb{q}_0^{-\top}$ . in the end, subtract  $\pmb{x}_{\star}\pmb{r}(\pmb{r}^{\top}\pmb{r})^{-1}\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2}$  from both sides to reach

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} - \mathcal {t} _ {\alpha} \left[ \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} + \boldsymbol {s} _ {\star} \right]\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} = \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = \left(l - l _ {\star}\right) \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} + l _ {\star} \left(r - r _ {\star}\right) ^ {\top} r \left(r ^ {\top} r\right) ^ {- 1} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = \pmb {\delta} _ {l} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} + \pmb {l} _ {\star} \pmb {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \pmb {r} (\pmb {r} ^ {\top} \pmb {r}) ^ {- 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

this finishes the proof.

# e proof for matrix completion

# e.1 new projection operator

# e.1.1 proof of proposition 1

first, notice that the optimization of  $\pmb{l}$  and  $\pmb{r}$  in (23) can be decomposed and done in parallel, hence we focus on the optimization of  $\pmb{l}$  below:

$$
\boldsymbol {l} = \operatorname * {a r g m i n} _ {\boldsymbol {l} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n _ {1} \times r}} \left\| (\boldsymbol {l} - \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}}) (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}}) ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \quad \text {s . t .} \quad \sqrt {n _ {1}} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}}) ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq b.
$$

by a change of variables as  $g \coloneqq l(\widetilde{r}^{\top}\widetilde{r})^{1 / 2}$  and  $\widetilde{g} \coloneqq \widetilde{l} (\widetilde{r}^{\top}\widetilde{r})^{1 / 2}$ , we rewrite the above problem equivalently as

$$
\boldsymbol {g} = \operatorname *{argmin}_{\boldsymbol {g}\in \mathbb{r}^{n_{1}\times r}}\| \boldsymbol {g} - \widetilde{\boldsymbol{g}}\|_{\mathsf{f}}^{2}\qquad \text{s.t.}\quad \sqrt{n_{1}}\left\| \boldsymbol {g}\right\|_{2,\infty}\leq b,
$$

whose solution is given as [cw15]

$$
\boldsymbol {g} _ {i, \cdot} = \left(1 \wedge \frac {b}{\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {g}} _ {i , \cdot} \| _ {2}}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {g}} _ {i, \cdot}, \quad 1 \leq i \leq n _ {1}.
$$

by applying again the change of variable  $\pmb{l} = \pmb{g}(\widetilde{\pmb{r}}^{\top}\widetilde{\pmb{r}})^{-1 / 2}$  and  $\widetilde{\pmb{l}} = \widetilde{\pmb{g}} (\widetilde{\pmb{r}}^{\top}\widetilde{\pmb{r}})^{-1 / 2}$ , we obtain the claimed solution.

# e.1.2 proof of lemma 6

we begin with proving the non-expansiveness property. denote the optimal alignment matrix between  $\widetilde{\pmb{f}}$  and  $\pmb{f}_{\star}$  as  $\widetilde{\pmb{q}}$ , whose existence is guaranteed by lemma 9. denoting  $\mathcal{p}_b(\widetilde{\pmb{f}}) = [\pmb{l}^\top, \pmb{r}^\top]^\top$ , by the definition of  $\mathrm{dist}(\mathcal{p}_b(\widetilde{\pmb{f}}), \pmb{f}_{\star})$ , we know that

$$
\left. \operatorname {d i s t} ^ {2} \left(\mathcal {p} _ {b} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}}), \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {i, \cdot} \tilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {i, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {n _ {2}} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} _ {j, \cdot} \tilde {\boldsymbol {q}} ^ {- \top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {j, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}. \right. \tag {66}
$$

recall that the condition  $\mathrm{dist}(\tilde{\pmb{f}},\pmb {f}_{\star})\leq \epsilon \sigma_r(\pmb {x}_\star)$  implies

$$
\left\| \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \vee \left\| \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \epsilon ,
$$

which, together with  $r_{\star}\sigma_{\star}^{-1 / 2} = v_{\star}$ , further implies that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i, \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {2} \leq \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i, \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2} \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} ^ {- \top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i, \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {v} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \left\| (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \leq (1 + \epsilon) \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i, \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

in addition, the  $\mu$ -incoherence of  $x_{\star}$  yields

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {i, i} \right\| _ {2} \leq \sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {u} _ {\star} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {1} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \leq \frac {b}{1 + \epsilon},
$$

where the last inequality follows from the choice of  $b$ . take the above two relations collectively to reach

$$
\frac {b}{\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i , \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2}} \geq \frac {\left\| (\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {i , \cdot} \right\| _ {2}}{\left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i , \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2}}.
$$

we claim that performing the following projection yields a contraction on each row; see also [zl16, lemma 11].

claim 5. for vectors  $\pmb{u},\pmb{u}_{\star}\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$  and  $\lambda \geq \| \pmb {u}_\star \| _2 / \| \pmb {u}\| _2$  , it holds that

$$
\left\| (1 \wedge \lambda) \boldsymbol {u} - \boldsymbol {u} _ {\star} \right\| _ {2} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {u} - \boldsymbol {u} _ {\star} \right\| _ {2}.
$$

apply claim 5 with  $\pmb{u} \coloneqq \widetilde{\pmb{l}}_{i, \cdot} \widetilde{\pmb{q}} \pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2}$ ,  $\pmb{u}_{\star} \coloneqq (\pmb{l}_{\star} \pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2})_{i, \cdot}$ , and  $\lambda \coloneqq b / (\sqrt{n_1} \| \widetilde{\pmb{l}}_{i, \cdot} \widetilde{\pmb{r}}^{\top} \|_2)$  to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {i, \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - (\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {i, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \left\| \left(1 \wedge \frac {b}{\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i , \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2}}\right) \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i, \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - (\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {i, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \left\| \tilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i, \cdot} \tilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}\right) _ {i, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

following a similar argument for  $\pmb{r}$ , and plugging them back to (66), we conclude that

$$
\mathrm {d i s t} ^ {2} (\mathcal {p} _ {b} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}}), \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}) \leq \sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n _ {1}} \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i, \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - (\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {i, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} + \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {n _ {2}} \left\| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {j, \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {q}} ^ {- \top} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - (\boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}) _ {j, \cdot} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \mathrm {d i s t} ^ {2} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}).
$$

we move on to the incoherence condition. for any  $1 \leq i \leq n_{1}$ , one has

$$
