\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {i, \cdot} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {n _ {2}} \langle \boldsymbol {l} _ {i, \cdot}, \boldsymbol {r} _ {j, \cdot} \rangle^ {2} = \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {n _ {2}} \left(1 \wedge \frac {b}{\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i , \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {2} \langle \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i, \cdot}, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {j, \cdot} \rangle^ {2} \left(1 \wedge \frac {b}{\sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {j , \cdot} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {2} \\ \stackrel {\mathrm {(i)}} {\leq} \left(1 \wedge \frac {b}{\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i ,} , \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {2} \sum_ {j = 1} ^ {n _ {2}} \langle \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i,}, \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {j,} \rangle^ {2} = \left(1 \wedge \frac {b}{\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i ,} , \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2}}\right) ^ {2} \| \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {i,}. \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} ^ {\top} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\stackrel {\mathrm {(i i)}} {\leq} \frac {b ^ {2}}{n _ {1}}.
$$

where (i) follows from  $1 \wedge \frac{b}{\sqrt{n_2} \| \tilde{\boldsymbol{r}}_{j, i} \tilde{\boldsymbol{l}}^\top \|_2} \leq 1$ , and (ii) follows from  $1 \wedge \frac{b}{\sqrt{n_1} \| \tilde{\boldsymbol{l}}_{i, i} \tilde{\boldsymbol{r}}^\top \|_2} \leq \frac{b}{\sqrt{n_1} \| \tilde{\boldsymbol{l}}_{i, i} \tilde{\boldsymbol{r}}^\top \|_2}$ . similarly, one has  $\| \boldsymbol{r}_j, \boldsymbol{l}^\top \|_2^2 \leq b^2 / n_2$ . combining these two bounds completes the proof.

proof of claim 5. when  $\lambda >1$ , the claim holds as an identity. otherwise  $\lambda \leq 1$ . denote  $h(\bar{\lambda})\coloneqq \| \bar{\lambda}\pmb {u} - \pmb{u}_{\star}\|_{2}^{2}$ . calculate its derivative to conclude that  $h(\bar{\lambda})$  is monotonically increasing when  $\bar{\lambda}\geq \lambda_{\star}\coloneqq \langle \pmb {u},\pmb{u}_{\star}\rangle /\| \pmb {u}\|_{2}^{2}$ . note that  $\lambda \geq \| \pmb{u}_{\star}\|_{2} / \| \pmb {u}\|_{2}\geq \lambda_{\star}$ , thus  $h(\lambda)\leq h(1)$ , i.e. the claim holds.

# e.2 proof of lemma 7

we first record two useful lemmas regarding the projector  $\mathcal{p}_{\omega}(\cdot)$ .

lemma 21 ([zl16, lemma 10]). suppose that  $x_{\star}$  is  $\mu$ -incoherent, and  $p \gtrsim \mu r \log (n_1 \vee n_2) / (n_1 \wedge n_2)$ . with overwhelming probability, one has

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left\langle (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {r} _ {a} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {l} _ {a} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top}\right), \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {r} _ {b} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {l} _ {b} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \right\rangle \right| \\ \leq c _ {1} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {r} _ {a} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {l} _ {a} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {r} _ {b} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {l} _ {b} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

simultaneously for all  $\pmb{l}_a, \pmb{l}_b \in \mathbb{r}^{n_1 \times r}$  and  $\pmb{r}_a, \pmb{r}_b \in \mathbb{r}^{n_2 \times r}$ , where  $c_1 > 0$  is some universal constant.

lemma 22 ([cl19, lemma 8], [cll20, lemma 12]). suppose that  $p \gtrsim \log (n_1 \vee n_2) / (n_1 \wedge n_2)$ . with overwhelming probability, one has

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \left\langle (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {a} \boldsymbol {r} _ {a} ^ {\top}\right), \boldsymbol {l} _ {b} \boldsymbol {r} _ {b} ^ {\top} \right\rangle \right| \\ \leq c _ {2} \sqrt {\frac {n _ {1} \lor n _ {2}}{p}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {a} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {b} \| _ {2, \infty} \land \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {a} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {b} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \left(\| \boldsymbol {r} _ {a} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {b} \| _ {2, \infty} \land \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {a} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {b} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

simultaneously for all  $\pmb{l}_a, \pmb{l}_b \in \mathbb{r}^{n_1 \times r}$  and  $\pmb{r}_a, \pmb{r}_b \in \mathbb{r}^{n_2 \times r}$ , where  $c_2 > 0$  is some universal constant.

in view of the above two lemmas, define the event  $\mathcal{e}$  as the intersection of the events that the bounds in lemmas 21 and 22 hold, which happens with overwhelming probability. the rest of the proof is then performed under the event that  $\mathcal{e}$  holds.

by the condition  $\mathrm{dist}(\pmb {f}_t,\pmb {f}_\star)\leq 0.02\sigma_r(\pmb {x}_\star)$  and lemma 9, one knows that  $q_{t}$ , the optimal alignment matrix between  $\pmb {f}_t$  and  $\pmb{f}_{\star}$  exists. therefore, for notational convenience, we denote  $\pmb {l}\coloneqq \pmb {l}_t\pmb {q}_t$ $\pmb {r}\coloneqq \pmb {r}_t\pmb {q}_t^{-\top}$ $\delta_l\coloneqq l - l_\star$ $\delta_r\coloneqq r - r_\star$  , and  $\epsilon \coloneqq 0.02$  . in addition, denote  $\widetilde{\pmb{f}}_{t + 1}$  as the update before projection as

$$
\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {t + 1} := \left[ \begin{array}{c} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {t + 1} \\ \widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {t + 1} \end{array} \right] = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \boldsymbol {l} _ {t} - \eta p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} (\boldsymbol {l} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} (\boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t}) ^ {- 1} \\ \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} - \eta p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} (\boldsymbol {l} _ {t} \boldsymbol {r} _ {t} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {l} _ {t} (\boldsymbol {l} _ {t} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {l} _ {t}) ^ {- 1} \end{array} \right],
$$

and therefore  $\pmb{f}_{t + 1} = \mathcal{p}_b(\bar{\pmb{f}}_{t + 1})$ . note that in view of lemma 6, it suffices to prove the following relation

$$
\operatorname {d i s t} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq (1 - 0. 6 \eta) \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right). \tag {67}
$$

the conclusion  $\| l_t r_t^\top - x_\star \|_{\mathbb{f}} \leq 1.5 \, \mathrm{dist}(f_t, f_\star)$  is a simple consequence of lemma 13; see (48) for a detailed argument. in what follows, we concentrate on proving (67).

to begin with, we list a few easy consequences under the assumed conditions.

claim 6. under conditions  $\mathrm{dist}(\pmb {f}_t,\pmb {f}_\star)\leq \epsilon \sigma_r(\pmb {x}_\star)$  and  $\sqrt{n_1}\| lr^{\top}\|_{2,\infty}\vee \sqrt{n_2}\| rl^{\top}\|_{2,\infty}\leq c_b\sqrt{\mu r}\sigma_1(\pmb {x}_\star),$  one has

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \vee \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \epsilon ; \tag {68a}
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{1 - \epsilon}; \tag {68b}
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}; \tag {68c}
$$

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \frac {c _ {b}}{1 - \epsilon} \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {1} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}); \tag {68d}
$$

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \frac {c _ {b} \kappa}{1 - \epsilon} \sqrt {\mu r}; \tag {68e}
$$

$$
\sqrt {n _ {1}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \vee \sqrt {n _ {2}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left(1 + \frac {c _ {b}}{1 - \epsilon}\right) \sqrt {\mu r} \sigma_ {1} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right). \tag {68f}
$$

now we are ready to embark on the proof of (67). by the definition of  $\mathrm{dist}(\widetilde{\pmb{f}}_{t + 1},\pmb {f}_{\star})$  , one has

$$
\left. \operatorname {d i s t} ^ {2} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \left\| \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2}, \right. \tag {69}
$$

where we recall that  $q_{t}$  is the optimal alignment matrix between  $\pmb{f}_{t}$  and  $\pmb{f}_{\star}$ . plug in the update rule (26) and the decomposition  $\pmb {l}\pmb{r}^{\top} - \pmb{x}_{\star} = \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} + \pmb{l}_{\star}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top}$  to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} = \left(\boldsymbol {l} - \eta p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} \left(\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = \pmb {\delta} _ {l} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta (\pmb {l} \pmb {r} ^ {\top} - \pmb {x} _ {\star}) \pmb {r} (\pmb {r} ^ {\top} \pmb {r}) ^ {- 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\pmb {l} \pmb {r} ^ {\top} - \pmb {x} _ {\star}) \pmb {r} (\pmb {r} ^ {\top} \pmb {r}) ^ {- 1} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = (1 - \eta) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

this allows us to expand the first square in (69) as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} = \underbrace {\left\| \left(1 - \eta\right) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {1}} \\ - 2 \eta (1 - \eta) \underbrace {\operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} ^ {\top}\right)} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {2}} \\ + 2 \eta^ {2} \underbrace {\operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} ^ {\top}\right)} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {3}} \\ + \eta^ {2} \underbrace {\left\| (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {4}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

in the sequel, we shall control the four terms separately, of which  $\mathfrak{p}_1$  is the main term, and  $\mathfrak{p}_2, \mathfrak{p}_3$  and  $\mathfrak{p}_4$  are perturbation terms.

1. notice that the main term  $\mathfrak{p}_1$  has already been controlled in (46) under the condition (68a). it obeys

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {1} \leq \left((1 - \eta) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta)\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

2. for the second term  $\mathfrak{p}_2$ , decompose  $\pmb{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} - \pmb{x}_{\star} = \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}_{\star}^{\top} + \pmb{l}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top}$  and apply the triangle inequality to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathfrak {p} _ {2} \right| = \left| \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} ^ {\top}\right) \right| \\ \leq \underbrace {\left| \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} ^ {\top}\right) \right|} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {2, 1}} \\ + \underbrace {\left| \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) \left(\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} ^ {\top}\right) \right|} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {2, 2}} \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
+ \underbrace {\left| \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} ^ {\top}\right) \right|}. _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {2, 3}}
$$

for the first term  $\mathfrak{p}_{2,1}$ , under the event  $\mathcal{e}$ , we can invoke lemma 21 to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {p} _ {2, 1} \leq c _ {1} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq c _ {1} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {f}} ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second line follows from the relation  $\| ab\|_{\mathsf{f}}\leq \| a\|_{\mathsf{op}}\| b\|_{\mathsf{f}}$  . use the condition (68c) to obtain

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {2, 1} \leq \frac {c _ {1}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

regarding the remaining terms  $\mathfrak{p}_{2,2}$  and  $\mathfrak{p}_{2,3}$ , our main hammer is lemma 22. invoking lemma 22 under the event  $\mathcal{e}$  with  $l_{a} := \delta_{l}\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2}$ ,  $r_{a} := r_{\star}\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{-1/2}$ ,  $l_{b} := \delta_{l}\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2}$ , and  $r_{b} := \delta_{r}(\pmb{r}^{\top}\pmb{r})^{-1}\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2}$ , we arrive at

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {p} _ {2, 2} \leq c _ {2} \sqrt {\frac {n _ {1} \lor n _ {2}}{p}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq c _ {2} \sqrt {\frac {n _ {1} \lor n _ {2}}{p}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {o p}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly, with the help of lemma 22, one has

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {2, 3} \leq c _ {2} \sqrt {\frac {n _ {1} \vee n _ {2}}{p}} \| l \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| r \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

utilizing the consequences in claim 6, we arrive at

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {2, 2} \leq \frac {c _ {2 k}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {b}}{1 - \epsilon}\right) \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {f}};
$$

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {2, 3} \leq \frac {c _ {2} c _ {b} ^ {2} \kappa^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {4}} \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}.
$$

we then combine the bounds for  $\mathfrak{p}_{2,1},\mathfrak{p}_{2,2}$  and  $\mathfrak{p}_{2,3}$  to see

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {p} _ {2} \leq \frac {c _ {1}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \lor n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \land n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2} \\ + \frac {c _ {2} \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {b}}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {c _ {b} ^ {2} \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\right) \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \\ = \delta_ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \delta_ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq (\delta_ {1} + \frac {\delta_ {2}}{2}) \| \pmb {\delta} _ {l} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {\delta_ {2}}{2} \| \pmb {\delta} _ {r} \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where we denote

$$
\delta_ {1} := \frac {c _ {1}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}}, \quad \mathrm {a n d} \quad \delta_ {2} := \frac {c _ {2} \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {b}}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {c _ {b} ^ {2} \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\right) \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}}.
$$

3. following a similar argument for controlling  $\mathfrak{p}_2$  (i.e. repeatedly using lemmas 21 and 22), we can obtain the following bounds for  $\mathfrak{p}_3$  and  $\mathfrak{p}_4$ , whose proof are deferred to the end of this section.

claim 7. under the event  $\mathcal{e}$ , one has

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {3} \leq \frac {\delta_ {2}}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + (\delta_ {1} + \frac {\delta_ {2}}{2}) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2};
$$

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {4} \leq \delta_ {1} \left(\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2} + \delta_ {2} \left(\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

taking the bounds for  $\mathfrak{p}_1, \mathfrak{p}_2, \mathfrak{p}_3$  and  $\mathfrak{p}_4$  collectively yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \leq \left((1 - \eta) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta (1 - \eta)\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \\ + \eta (1 - \eta) \left((2 \delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \delta_ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \eta^ {2} \left(\delta_ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + (2 \delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + \eta^ {2} \left(\delta_ {1} (\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \delta_ {2} (\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

a similar upper bound holds for the second square in (69). as a result, we reach the conclusion that

$$
\left\| \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\tilde {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \leq \rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \delta_ {1}, \delta_ {2}) \operatorname {d i s t} ^ {2} (\boldsymbol {f} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}),
$$

where the contraction rate  $\rho^2 (\eta ;\epsilon ,\delta_1,\delta_2)$  is given by

$$
\rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \delta_ {1}, \delta_ {2}) := (1 - \eta) ^ {2} + \left(\frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + 2 (\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2})\right) \eta (1 - \eta) + \left(\frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} + 2 (\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}) + (\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}) ^ {2}\right) \eta^ {2}.
$$

as long as  $p \geq c(\mu r\kappa^4 \vee \log (n_1 \vee n_2))\mu r / (n_1 \wedge n_2)$  for some sufficiently large constant  $c$ , one has  $\delta_1 + \delta_2 \leq 0.1$  under the setting  $\epsilon = 0.02$ . when  $0 < \eta \leq 2/3$ , one further has  $\rho (\eta; \epsilon, \delta_1, \delta_2) \leq 1 - 0.6\eta$ . thus we conclude that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \sqrt {\left\| \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {r}} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} \\ \leq (1 - 0. 6 \eta) \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

which is exactly the upper bound we are after; see (67). this finishes the proof.

proof of claim 6. first, repeating the derivation for (45) obtains (68a). second, take the condition (68a) and lemma 12 together to obtain (68b) and (68c). third, take the incoherence condition  $\sqrt{n_1}\| lr^\top \|_{2,\infty} \vee \sqrt{n_2}\| rl^\top \|_{2,\infty} \leq c_b\sqrt{\mu r}\sigma_1(x_\star)$  together with the relations

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \| _ {2, \infty} \geq \sigma_ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \\ \geq \left(\sigma_ {r} \left(r _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) - \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \\ \geq (1 - \epsilon) \| \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty}; \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {l} ^ {\top} \| _ {2, \infty} \geq \sigma_ {r} \left(\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \\ \geq \left(\sigma_ {r} \left(l _ {\star} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) - \| \delta_ {l} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}}\right) \| r \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \\ \geq (1 - \epsilon) \| r \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \\ \end{array}
$$

to obtain (68d) and (68e). finally, apply the triangle inequality together with incoherence assumption to obtain (68f).

proof of claim 7. we start with the term  $\mathfrak{p}_3$ , for which we have

$$
| \mathfrak {p} _ {3} | \leq \underbrace {\left. \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top}\right) \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} ^ {\top}\right) \right|} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {3, 1}}
$$

$$
+ \underbrace {\left| \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} ^ {\top}\right) \right|}.
$$

invoke lemma 21 to bound  $\mathfrak{p}_{3,1}$  as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {p} _ {3, 1} \leq c _ {1} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| l _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| l _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq c _ {1} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

the condition (68b) allows us to obtain a simplified bound

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {3, 1} \leq \frac {c _ {1}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

in regard to  $\mathfrak{p}_{3,2}$ , we apply lemma 22 with  $l_{a} \coloneqq \delta_{l} \sigma_{\star}^{1/2}$ ,  $r_{a} \coloneqq r \sigma_{\star}^{-1/2}$ ,  $l_{b} \coloneqq l_{\star} \sigma_{\star}^{-1/2}$ , and  $r_{b} \coloneqq r(r^{\top} r)^{-1} \sigma_{\star}(r^{\top} r)^{-1} r^{\top} \delta_{r} \sigma_{\star}^{1/2}$  to see

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {p} _ {3, 2} \leq c _ {2} \sqrt {\frac {n _ {1} \vee n _ {2}}{p}} \| \delta_ {l} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| l _ {\star} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| r \sigma_ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| r (r ^ {\top} r) ^ {- 1} \sigma_ {\star} (r ^ {\top} r) ^ {- 1} r ^ {\top} \delta_ {r} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq c _ {2} \sqrt {\frac {n _ {1} \vee n _ {2}}{p}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

again, use the consequences in claim 6 to reach

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {p} _ {3, 2} \leq c _ {2} \sqrt {\frac {n _ {1} \vee n _ {2}}{p}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \frac {c _ {b} \kappa}{1 - \epsilon} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {2}}} \frac {1}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \\ = \frac {c _ {2} c _ {b} \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {3}} \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

combine the bounds of  $\mathfrak{p}_{3,1}$  and  $\mathfrak{p}_{3,2}$  to reach

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathfrak {p} _ {3} \leq \frac {c _ {1}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \delta_ {r} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} ^ {2} \\ + \frac {c _ {2} c _ {b} \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {3}} \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq \delta_ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \delta_ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ \leq \frac {\delta_ {2}}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + (\delta_ {1} + \frac {\delta_ {2}}{2}) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

moving on to the term  $\mathfrak{p}_4$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sqrt {\mathfrak {p} _ {4}} = \left\| (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {f} \\ \leq \underbrace {\left| \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} ^ {\top}\right) \right|} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {4, 1}} \\ + \underbrace {\left| \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} ^ {\top}\right) \right|} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {4, 2}} \\ + \underbrace {\left| \operatorname {t r} \left((p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \widetilde {\boldsymbol {l}} ^ {\top}\right) \right| ,} _ {\mathfrak {p} _ {4, 3}} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we have used the variational representation of the frobenius norm for some  $\widetilde{\pmb{l}}\in \mathbb{r}^{n_1\times r}$  obeying  $\| \widetilde{l}\|_{\mathsf{f}} = 1$ . note that the decomposition of  $\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}_4}$  is extremely similar to that of  $\mathfrak{p}_2$ . therefore we can follow a similar argument (i.e. applying lemmas 21 and 22) to control these terms as

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {4, 1} \leq \frac {c _ {1}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \sqrt {\frac {\mu r \log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}};
$$

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {4, 2} \leq \frac {c _ {2} \kappa}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \left(1 + \frac {c _ {b}}{1 - \epsilon}\right) \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}};
$$

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {4, 3} \leq \frac {c _ {2} c _ {b} ^ {2} \kappa^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {4}} \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {p (n _ {1} \wedge n _ {2})}} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}.
$$

for conciseness, we omit the details for bounding each term. combine them to reach

$$
\sqrt {\mathfrak {p} _ {4}} \leq \delta_ {1} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \delta_ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}.
$$

finally take the square on both sides and use  $2ab \leq a^2 + b^2$  to obtain the upper bound

$$
\mathfrak {p} _ {4} \leq \delta_ {1} (\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \delta_ {2} (\delta_ {1} + \delta_ {2}) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

# e.3 proof of lemma 8

we start by recording a useful lemma below.

lemma 23 ([che15, lemma 2], [cll20, lemma 4]). for any fixed  $x \in \mathbb{r}^{n_1 \times n_2}$ , with overwhelming probability, one has

$$
\left\| (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {x}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c _ {0} \frac {\log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p} \| \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {\infty} + c _ {0} \sqrt {\frac {\log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p}} (\| \boldsymbol {x} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \| \boldsymbol {x} ^ {\top} \| _ {2, \infty}),
$$

where  $c_0 > 0$  is some universal constant that does not depend on  $\pmb{x}$

in view of lemma 11, one has

$$
\left. \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {0}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \sqrt {\sqrt {2} + 1} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {0} \boldsymbol {v} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}} \leq \sqrt {(\sqrt {2} + 1) 2 r} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {0} \boldsymbol {v} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}, \right. \tag {70}
$$

where the last relation uses the fact that  $u_0 \sigma_0 v_0^\top - x_\star$  has rank at most  $2r$ . applying the triangle inequality, we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \boldsymbol {u} _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {0} \boldsymbol {v} _ {0} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) - \boldsymbol {u} _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {0} \boldsymbol {v} _ {0} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} + \left\| p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \\ \leq 2 \left\| \left(p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}\right) \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}. \tag {71} \\ \end{array}
$$

here the second inequality hinges on the fact that  $\pmb{u}_0\pmb{\sigma}_0\pmb{v}_0^\top$  is the best rank-  $r$  approximation to  $p^{-1}\mathcal{p}_{\omega}(\pmb{x}_{\star})$ , i.e.

$$
\left\| p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) - \boldsymbol {u} _ {0} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {0} \boldsymbol {v} _ {0} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \left\| p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}\right) - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

combining (70) and (71) yields

$$
\mathrm {d i s t} (\widetilde {\boldsymbol {f}} _ {0}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}) \leq 2 \sqrt {(\sqrt {2} + 1) 2 r} \left\| (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq 5 \sqrt {r} \left\| (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}}.
$$

it then boils down to controlling  $\left\| p^{-1}\mathcal{p}_{\omega}(\pmb{x}_{\star}) - \pmb{x}_{\star}\right\|_{\mathrm{op}}$ , which is readily supplied by lemma 23 as

$$
\left\| (p ^ {- 1} \mathcal {p} _ {\omega} - \mathcal {i}) (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq c _ {0} \frac {\log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p} \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\infty} + c _ {0} \sqrt {\frac {\log (n _ {1} \vee n _ {2})}{p}} (\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {2, \infty} \vee \| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \| _ {2, \infty}),
$$

which holds with overwhelming probability. the proof is finished by plugging the following bounds from incoherence assumption of  $x_{\star}$ :

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\infty} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {u} _ {\star} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} _ {\star} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \frac {\mu r}{\sqrt {n _ {1} n _ {2}}} \kappa \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star});
$$

$$
\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {2, \infty} \leq \| \boldsymbol {u} _ {\star} \| _ {2, \infty} \| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \| \boldsymbol {v} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {1}}} \kappa \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star});
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \left\| \boldsymbol {u} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \left\| \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \left\| \boldsymbol {v} _ {\star} \right\| _ {2, \infty} \leq \sqrt {\frac {\mu r}{n _ {2}}} \kappa \sigma_ {r} (\boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}).
$$

# f proof for general loss functions

we first present a useful property of restricted smooth and convex functions.

lemma 24. suppose that  $f: \mathbb{r}^{n_1 \times n_2} \mapsto \mathbb{r}$  is rank-2r restricted  $l$ -smooth and rank-2r restricted convex. then for any  $x_1, x_2 \in \mathbb{r}^{n_1 \times n_2}$  of rank at most  $r$ , one has

$$
\langle \nabla f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}) - \nabla f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}), \boldsymbol {x} _ {1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} \rangle \geq \frac {1}{l} \| \nabla f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}) - \nabla f (\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} ^ {2}.
$$

proof. since  $f(\cdot)$  is rank-2r restricted  $l$ -smooth and convex, it holds for any  $\bar{\pmb{x}} \in \mathbb{r}^{n_1 \times n_2}$  with rank at most 2r that

$$
f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right) + \langle \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right), \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {1} \rangle \leq f (\bar {\boldsymbol {x}}) \leq f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right) + \langle \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right), \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} \rangle + \frac {l}{2} \| \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} \| _ {\mathrm {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

reorganize the terms to yield

$$
f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right) + \langle \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right), \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {1} \rangle \leq f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right) + \langle \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right) - \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right), \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} \rangle + \frac {l}{2} \| \bar {\boldsymbol {x}} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} \| _ {\mathbf {f}} ^ {2}.
$$

take  $\bar{\pmb{x}} = \pmb{x}_2 - \frac{1}{l}\mathcal{p}_r(\nabla f(\pmb{x}_2) - \nabla f(\pmb{x}_1))$ , whose rank is at most  $2r$ , to see

$$
f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right) + \langle \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right), \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {1} \rangle + \frac {1}{2 l} \| \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right) - \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right) \| _ {\mathrm {f}, r} ^ {2} \leq f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right).
$$

we can further switch the roles of  $x_{1}$  and  $x_{2}$  to obtain

$$
f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right) + \left\langle \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right), \boldsymbol {x} _ {1} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {2} \right\rangle + \frac {1}{2 l} \| \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {2}\right) - \nabla f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right) \| _ {\mathrm {f}, r} ^ {2} \leq f \left(\boldsymbol {x} _ {1}\right).
$$

adding the above two inequalities yields the desired bound.

# f.1 proof of theorem 4

suppose that the  $t$ -th iterate  $\pmb{f}_t$  obeys the condition  $\mathrm{dist}(\pmb{f}_t, \pmb{f}_{\star}) \leq 0.1 \sigma_r(\pmb{x}_{\star}) / \sqrt{\kappa_f}$ . in view of lemma 9, one knows that  $\pmb{q}_t$ , the optimal alignment matrix between  $\pmb{f}_t$  and  $\pmb{f}_{\star}$  exists. therefore, for notational convenience, denote  $\pmb{l} \coloneqq \pmb{l}_t \pmb{q}_t$ ,  $\pmb{r} \coloneqq \pmb{r}_t \pmb{q}_t^{-\top}$ ,  $\delta_l \coloneqq \pmb{l} - \pmb{l}_{\star}$ ,  $\delta_r \coloneqq \pmb{r} - \pmb{r}_{\star}$ , and  $\epsilon \coloneqq 0.1 / \sqrt{\kappa_f}$ . similar to the derivation in (45), we have

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \vee \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \epsilon . \tag {72}
$$

the conclusion  $\| l_t r_t^\top - x_\star \|_{\mathbb{f}} \leq 1.5 \, \mathrm{dist}(f_t, f_\star)$  is a simple consequence of lemma 13; see (48) for a detailed argument. from now on, we focus on proving the distance contraction.

by the definition of  $\mathrm{dist}(\pmb {f}_{t + 1},\pmb {f}_\star)$  , one has

$$
\left. \operatorname {d i s t} ^ {2} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}. \right. \tag {73}
$$

introduce an auxiliary function

$$
f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {x}) = f (\boldsymbol {x}) - \frac {\mu}{2} \| \boldsymbol {x} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2},
$$

which is rank-  $2r$  restricted  $(l - \mu)$ -smooth and rank-  $2r$  restricted convex. using the scaledgd update rule (27) and the decomposition  $\pmb{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} - \pmb{x}_{\star} = \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}^{\top} + \pmb{l}_{\star}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top}$ , we obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} = \left(\boldsymbol {l} - \eta \nabla f (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = \left(\boldsymbol {l} - \eta \mu (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} - \eta \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \\ = (1 - \eta \mu) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta \mu \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l r} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

as a result, one can expand the first square in (73) as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} = \underbrace {\left\| (1 - \eta \mu) \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \eta \mu \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} _ {\mathfrak {g} _ {1}} \\ - 2 \eta (1 - \eta \mu) \underbrace {\left\langle \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) , \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} - \frac {1}{2} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \right\rangle} _ {\mathfrak {g} _ {2}} \\ - 2 \eta (1 - \eta \mu) \left\langle \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}), \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} + \frac {1}{2} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \right\rangle \\ + 2 \eta^ {2} \mu \underbrace {\left\langle \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) , \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \right\rangle} _ {\mathfrak {g} _ {3}} \\ + \eta^ {2} \underbrace {\left\| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} _ {\mathfrak {g} _ {4}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

in the sequel, we shall bound the four terms separately.

1. notice that the main term  $\mathfrak{g}_1$  has already been controlled in (46) under the condition (72). it obeys

$$
\mathfrak {g} _ {1} \leq \left((1 - \eta \mu) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta \mu (1 - \eta \mu)\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2},
$$

as long as  $\eta \mu \leq 2 / 3$

2. for the second term  $\mathfrak{g}_2$ , note that  $\pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{\sigma}_{\star}(\pmb{r}^{\top}\pmb{r})^{-1}\pmb{r}^{\top} - \pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{r}_{\star}^{\top} - \frac{1}{2}\pmb{\delta}_{l}\pmb{\delta}_{r}^{\top}$  has rank at most  $r$ . hence we can invoke lemma 15 to obtain

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathfrak {g} _ {2} \right| \leq \left\| \nabla f _ {\mu} \left(\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}, r} \left\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} - \frac {1}{2} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}} \\ \leq \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \left(\left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \boldsymbol {v} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathsf {o p}} + \frac {1}{2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {o p}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second line uses  $r_{\star} = v_{\star}\sigma_{\star}^{1 / 2}$ . take the condition (72) and lemma 12 together to obtain

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {1}{1 - \epsilon};
$$

$$
\left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \sigma_ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} - \boldsymbol {v} _ {\star} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} \leq \frac {\sqrt {2} \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon}.
$$

these consequences further imply that

$$
| \mathfrak {g} _ {2} | \leq (\frac {\sqrt {2} \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {\epsilon}{2}) \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}.
$$

3. as above, the third term  $\mathfrak{g}_3$  can be similarly bounded as

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| \mathfrak {g} _ {3} \right| \leq \left\| \nabla f _ {\mu} \left(\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}\right) \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}, r} \left\| \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} \left(\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}\right) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \right\| _ {\mathrm {f}} \\ \leq \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathbb {f}, r} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathbb {f}} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathrm {o p}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {1}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

4. for the last term  $\mathfrak{g}_4$ , invoke lemma 15 to obtain

$$
\mathfrak {g} _ {4} \leq \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} ^ {2} \left\| \boldsymbol {r} (\boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} \boldsymbol {r}) ^ {- 1} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {o p}} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} ^ {2}.
$$

taking collectively the bounds for  $\mathfrak{g}_1, \mathfrak{g}_2, \mathfrak{g}_3$  and  $\mathfrak{g}_4$  yields

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \leq \left((1 - \eta \mu) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta \mu (1 - \eta \mu)\right) \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \\ + 2 \eta \left(\frac {\sqrt {2} \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {\epsilon}{2}\right) \left(1 - \eta \mu\right) \| \nabla f _ {\mu} \left(\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}\right) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} \\ - 2 \eta (1 - \eta \mu) \left\langle \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}), \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} + \frac {1}{2} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \right\rangle \\ + \frac {2 \eta^ {2} \mu}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \frac {\eta^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly, we can obtain the control of  $\| (\pmb{r}_{t + 1}\pmb{q}_t^{-\top} - \pmb{r}_\star)\pmb{\sigma}_\star^{1 / 2}\|_{\mathbb{f}}^2$ . combine them together to reach

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \left((1 - \eta \mu) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta \mu (1 - \eta \mu) + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2}\right) \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + 2 \eta \left(\left(\frac {\sqrt {2} \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {\epsilon}{2}\right) (1 - \eta \mu) + \frac {\eta \mu}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\right) \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \\ - 2 \eta (1 - \eta \mu) \left\langle \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}), \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \right\rangle + \frac {2 \eta^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathbb {f}, r} ^ {2} \\ \leq \left((1 - \eta \mu) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta \mu (1 - \eta \mu) + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2}\right) \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right) \\ + 2 \eta \underbrace {\left(\frac {\sqrt {2} \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {\epsilon}{2}\right) (1 - \eta \mu) + \frac {\eta \mu}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\left. \right)} _ {\mathfrak {c} _ {1}} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) \\ - 2 \eta \underbrace {\left(\frac {1 - \eta \mu}{l - \mu} - \frac {\eta}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\right)} _ {\mathfrak {c} _ {2}} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last line follows from lemma 24 (notice that  $\nabla f_{\mu}(\pmb{x}_{\star}) = \mathbf{0}$ ) as

$$
\langle \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}), \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} + \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} ^ {\top} \rangle = \langle \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}), \boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top} - \boldsymbol {x} _ {\star} \rangle \geq \frac {1}{l - \mu} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathbb {f}, r} ^ {2}.
$$

notice that  $\mathfrak{c}_2 > 0$  as long as  $\eta \leq (1 - \epsilon)^2 /l$ . maximizing the quadratic function of  $\| \nabla f_{\mu}(\pmb {l}\pmb{r}^{\top})\|_{\mathrm{f},r}$  yields

$$
\mathfrak {c} _ {1} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) - \mathfrak {c} _ {2} \| \nabla f _ {\mu} (\boldsymbol {l} \boldsymbol {r} ^ {\top}) \| _ {\mathsf {f}, r} ^ {2} \leq \frac {\mathfrak {c} _ {1} ^ {2}}{4 \mathfrak {c} _ {2}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}}\right) ^ {2}
$$

$$
\leq \frac {\mathfrak {c} _ {1} ^ {2}}{2 \mathfrak {c} _ {2}} \left(\| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {l} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \| \boldsymbol {\delta} _ {r} \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}\right),
$$

where the last inequality holds since  $(a + b)^2 \leq 2(a^2 + b^2)$ . identify  $\mathrm{dist}^2(\pmb{f}_t, \pmb{f}_{\star}) = \|\pmb{\delta}_l \pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2}\|_{\mathbb{f}}^2 + \|\pmb{\delta}_r \pmb{\sigma}_{\star}^{1/2}\|_{\mathbb{f}}^2$  to obtain

$$
\left\| (\pmb {l} _ {t + 1} \pmb {q} _ {t} - \pmb {l} _ {\star}) \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| (\pmb {r} _ {t + 1} \pmb {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \pmb {r} _ {\star}) \pmb {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} \leq \rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \mu , l) \mathrm {d i s t} ^ {2} (\pmb {f} _ {t}, \pmb {f} _ {\star}),
$$

where the contraction rate is given by

$$
\rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \mu , l) := (1 - \eta \mu) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta \mu (1 - \eta \mu) + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2} + \frac {\left((\frac {\sqrt {2} \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {\epsilon}{2}) (1 - \eta \mu) + \frac {\eta \mu}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\right) ^ {2}}{1 - \eta \mu - \frac {\eta (l - \mu)}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}} \eta (l - \mu).
$$

with  $\epsilon = 0.1 / \sqrt{\kappa_f}$  and  $0 < \eta \leq 0.4 / l$ , one has  $\rho (\eta ;\epsilon ,\mu ,l)\leq 1 - 0.7\eta \mu$ . thus we conclude that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t + 1}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right) \leq \sqrt {\left\| \left(\boldsymbol {l} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} - \boldsymbol {l} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2} + \left\| \left(\boldsymbol {r} _ {t + 1} \boldsymbol {q} _ {t} ^ {- \top} - \boldsymbol {r} _ {\star}\right) \boldsymbol {\sigma} _ {\star} ^ {1 / 2} \right\| _ {\mathsf {f}} ^ {2}} \\ \leq (1 - 0. 7 \eta \mu) \operatorname {d i s t} \left(\boldsymbol {f} _ {t}, \boldsymbol {f} _ {\star}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

which is the desired claim.

remark 4. we provide numerical details for the contraction rate. for simplicity, we shall prove  $\rho (\eta ;\epsilon ,\mu ,l)\leq 1 - 0.7\eta \mu$  under a stricter condition  $\epsilon = 0.02 / \sqrt{\kappa_f}$ . the stronger result under the condition  $\epsilon = 0.1 / \sqrt{\kappa_f}$  can be verified through a subtler analysis.

with  $\epsilon = 0.02 / \sqrt{\kappa_f}$  and  $0 < \eta \leq 0.4 / l$ , one can bound the terms in  $\rho^2 (\eta ;\epsilon ,\mu ,l)$  as

$$
(1 - \eta \mu) ^ {2} + \frac {2 \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} \eta \mu (1 - \eta \mu) + \frac {2 \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}} \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2} \leq 1 - 1. 9 5 9 \eta \mu + 1. 0 0 2 \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2}; \tag {74}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \frac {\left(\left(\frac {\sqrt {2} \epsilon}{1 - \epsilon} + \frac {\epsilon}{2}\right) (1 - \eta \mu) + \frac {\eta \mu}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}\right) ^ {2}}{1 - \eta \mu - \frac {\eta (l - \mu)}{(1 - \epsilon) ^ {2}}} \eta (l - \mu) \leq \frac {\frac {0 . 0 0 1 6}{\kappa_ {f}} + 0 . 0 7 8 \eta \mu + 1 . 0 0 5 \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2}}{1 - 1 . 0 4 2 \eta l} \eta l \\ \leq \frac {0 . 0 0 1 6 \eta \frac {l}{\kappa_ {f}} + 0 . 4 \times (0 . 0 7 8 \eta \mu + 1 . 0 0 5 \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2})}{1 - 0 . 4 \times 1 . 0 4 2} \\ \leq 0. 0 5 7 \eta \mu + 0. 6 9 \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2}, \tag {75} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last line uses the definition (28) of  $\kappa_{f}$ . putting (74) and (75) together further implies

$$
\rho^ {2} (\eta ; \epsilon , \mu , l) \leq 1 - 1. 9 \eta \mu + 1. 7 \eta^ {2} \mu^ {2} \leq (1 - 0. 7 \eta \mu) ^ {2},
$$

as long as  $0 < \eta \mu \leq 0.4$ .
